          CHAPTER 2

                            Section Il. CONTROLS       AND INSTRUMENTS

18. General                                            ment is discussed in section III of this chapter.

   Haphazard operation or improper setting of the      19. Controls and Their Uses
controls can cause damage to the signal generator.             (fig. 7)
To prevent such damage, it is important to know
the function of all the front panel controls, meters,     The following chart lists the controls, meters,
and terminals. The actual operation of the equip-      and terminations on the front panel of the signal
                                                       generator and indicates their functions.

            Control                                                    Function

CAUTION: pla22t 2 5e 2 5: -2 Soe    | Covers toggle switch which permits either. 115- or 230-volt operation.

POWER ON-OFF (S86) switch _______-  Controls the a-ec line voltage to the signal generator. In the OFF

                                    position, internal resistor-type heaters turn on to keep the signal

                                                                                generator warm and dry, provided the line is plugged in.

POWER ON (I 7) (pilot light)...__________- | This red pilot lamp lights when the POWER switch is ON and line

                                    voltage is applied to the power transformer.

HEATER ON (I 6) (pilot light)____________- This neon bulb lights when the resistor-type internal heaters are on.

BAND S WIT CHS Ss) 2 oo s2e)eee     Selects the band containing the desired frequency in the range of 20 to

                                    100 me. A dial mask allows just one band at a time to be visible.

                                    There are five positions of the switch (A through E).

RE TUNING controle sss oe See eee   Tunes the desired frequency in each band selected by the BAND

                                    SWITCH. A dial lock is provided to hold the setting, once tuned.

CORRBCTORMCZ) Sea eer , .           Trims the oscillator frequency so that it corresponds to the frequency

                                    indicated on the dial scale. It is used in conjunction with the internal

                                    crystal calibrator.

DEVIATION RANGE switch (S83) 25-50-100_| Selects one of three different frequency deviation ranges when an f-m

                                    output signal is used. The three ranges are 0 to 25 ke, 0 to 50 ke, and

                                    0 to 100 ke.

DEVIATION LEVEL control (R49)_________ | Adjusts the amount of frequency deviation within each of the three

                                    deviation ranges.

KILOCYCLES DEVIATION meter (M2)____ This meter has three ranges corresponding to the ranges available with

                                    settings of the DEVIATION RANGE switch.

OPERATIONsswitch (Steen  2 eee      Determines the operation of the signal generator. It has the following

                                    6 positions:

                                    B+ OFF In this position, all high voltage is removed from the

                                                         plate circuits, thereby disabling the signal generator.

                                    CAL                This position activates the 20- to 100-me r-f section

                                                         and places the calibration circuits in operation to

                                                         provide check points for accurate calibration of the

                                                         tuning dial.

                                    MOD OFF In this position, the signal generator output is unmodu-

                                                         lated and has the frequency range of 20 to 100 me.

                                    1000               Provides an f-m output modulated at a 1,000-cps rate

                                                         in the frequency range of 20 to 100 me.

                                    EXT MOD In this position, the internal 1,000-cps modulating

                                                         oscillator is disconnected and the r-f oscillator can

                                                         be modulated by an external source at the EXT

                                                         MOD input terminals.

                                    IF                 In this position, the high voltage is connected to only

                                                         the i-f oscillator and amplifier circuits. This pro-

                                                         vides unmodulated ecrystal-controlled i-f output sig-

                                                         nals at the i-f output lead.

IF FREQUENCY MC switch (S8).---__-_.. Selects the proper crystals and tuning circuits to provide the following

                                    output frequencies: 1.4, 2.515, 2.65, 2.88, 4.3, 4.45, 5.00, 5.35, and

                                    15.0 me. Two spare positions are provided for future use.

I. F. MICROVOLTS, R. F. SET TO LINE | Used to indicate that the proper r-f voltage level is being fed into the

    meter.                          r-f attenuator. Also used to indicate the i-f output voltage (the

                                    scale numbers, when multiplied by the IF MICROVOLTS attenuator

                                    setting, are the i-f output voltages).

                                                         25. Stopping Procedure

                                                            To stop the signal generator, place the POWER
                                                         switch in the OFF position. The POWER ON
                                                         pilot light will go off and the HEATER ON neon
                                                         pilot light willlight. Turn the RF SET TO LINE
                                                         and IF LEVEL controls to their extreme counter-
                                                         clockwise positions. Turn the OPERATION

                                                         switch to the B+ OFF position.

 74. Adjustment of RF TUNING Dial Clutch
                                                         76. Adjustment of Dial Mask and Switch S2
    There is a clutch on the vernier knob for the RF

SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE AND DEMOLITION TO PREVENT

                                      ENEMY USE

103. Repacking for Shipment or Limited               or salvaging this equipment. Demolition of the
          Storage                                    equipment will be accomplished only on order of
                                                     the commander.
   a. The exact procedure used in repacking for
shipment or limited storage depends on the ma-       105. Methods of Destruction
terial available and the conditions under which the
equipment is to be shipped or stored. If the            a. Smash. Smash the crystals, controls, tubes,
original packing materials have been saved or if     coils, switches, capacitors, and transformers, using
similar materials are available, refer to paragraph  sledges, axes, handaxes, hammers, crowbars, or
13 for repacking.                                    other heavy tools.

   b. Whenever practicable, place a dehydrating         b. Cut. Cut cords and wiring, using axes,
agent, such as silica gel, inside the inner carton.  handaxes, or machetes.
Protect the transit case with a waterproof paper
barrier. Seal the seams of the paper barrier with       c. Burn. Burncords, resistors, capacitors, coils,
waterproof sealing compound or tape. Pack the        wiring, and technical manuals, using gasoline,
protected case in a padded wooden case, providing    kerosene, oil, flame throwers, or incendiary
at least 3 inches of excelsior padding between the   grenades.
paper barrier and the packing case.
                                                        d. Bend. Bend panels, cabinet, and chassis.
104. Demolition of Materiel to Prevent Enemy            e. Explosives. If explosives are necessary, use
          Use                                        firearms, grenades, or TNT.

   The demolition procedures outlined in paragraph     f. Disposal. Bury or scatter the destroyed
105 will be used to prevent the enemy from using     parts in slit trenches, fox holes, or other holes, or

the Signal portion of the Department of the Army Supply Catalog, see the latest issue of SIG 1, Intro-
